# Third-party notices

Generated by `go run ./scripts/licenses`. Do not edit by hand. Verify with `go run ./scripts/licenses -check`.

Inventory: union of native `go list -deps -test -json ./...` and `go list -deps -json ./cmd/looster` for linux, darwin, windows × amd64, arm64 with CGO_ENABLED=0. Build means used by a release target. Test-only means present only in the native test graph (which also includes native package builds and the generator). No claim is made about other build tags or CGO releases.

All matching license, copying, notice, authors and copyright files are reproduced recursively from used module directories, including bundled material and fixtures. Classifications are conservative content heuristics, not legal advice. Unrecognized license documents fail generation and require explicit review. Fully identified combined texts are marked for review. Supplemental attribution, patent and notice texts are labeled separately. The exact reviewed Lucent license is accepted only by its pinned SHA-256, not mislabeled as ISC. Source ZIP links provide the exact versioned module source, including MPL-covered source.

## Go toolchain go1.27.2

Source: https://go.dev/dl/ (exact version go1.27.2). Toolchain LICENSE and PATENTS, plus notices beneath used standard-library package directories and toolchain third_party directories.

Classification: BSD-3-Clause (content match, review scope)

```text
The Go source code and supporting files in this directory
are covered by the usual Go license (see ../../../../LICENSE).

When building with GOEXPERIMENT=boringcrypto, the following applies.

The goboringcrypto_linux_amd64.syso object file is built
from BoringSSL source code by build/build.sh and is covered
by the BoringSSL license reproduced below and also at
https://boringssl.googlesource.com/boringssl/+/fips-20220613/LICENSE.

BoringSSL is a fork of OpenSSL. As such, large parts of it fall under OpenSSL
licensing. Files that are completely new have a Google copyright and an ISC
license. This license is reproduced at the bottom of this file.

Contributors to BoringSSL are required to follow the CLA rules for Chromium:
https://cla.developers.google.com/clas

Some files from Intel are under yet another license, which is also included
underneath.

The OpenSSL toolkit stays under a dual license, i.e. both the conditions of the
OpenSSL License and the original SSLeay license apply to the toolkit. See below
for the actual license texts. Actually both licenses are BSD-style Open Source
licenses. In case of any license issues related to OpenSSL please contact
openssl-core@openssl.org.

The following are Google-internal bug numbers where explicit permission from
some authors is recorded for use of their work. (This is purely for our own
record keeping.)

  27287880
  27287883
```

## github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2@v1.43.4

Usage: **build**. Inventories: darwin/amd64, darwin/arm64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64, native tests, windows/amd64, windows/arm64.

Source ZIP: https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2/@v/v1.43.4.zip

### `.github/workflows/license-check.yml`

Classification: Supplemental matched source / metadata (not a license document)

```text
name: License Scan

on: [pull_request]


permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  licensescan:
    name: License Scan
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      matrix:
        python-version: [3.9]

    steps:
      - name: Checkout target
        uses: actions/checkout@v2
        with:
          path: sdkbase
          ref: ${{ github.base_ref }}
      - name: Checkout this ref
        uses: actions/checkout@v2
        with:
          path: new-ref
          fetch-depth: 0
      - name: Get Diff
        run: git --git-dir ./new-ref/.git diff --name-only --diff-filter=ACMRT ${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha }} ${{ github.sha }} > refDiffFiles.txt
      - name: Get Target Files
        run: git --git-dir ./sdkbase/.git ls-files | grep -xf refDiffFiles.txt - > targetFiles.txt
      - name: Checkout scancode
        uses: actions/checkout@v2
        with:
          repository: nexB/scancode-toolkit
          path: scancode-toolkit
          fetch-depth: 1
      - name: Set up Python ${{ matrix.python-version }}
        uses: actions/setup-python@v2
        with:
          python-version: ${{ matrix.python-version }}
      # ScanCode
      - name: Self-configure scancode
        working-directory: ./scancode-toolkit
        run: ./scancode --help
      - name: Run Scan code on target
        run: cat targetFiles.txt | while read filename; do echo ./sdkbase/$filename; done | xargs ./scancode-toolkit/scancode -l -n 30 --json-pp - | grep short_name | sort | uniq >> old-licenses.txt
      - name: Run Scan code on pr ref
        run: cat refDiffFiles.txt | while read filename; do echo ./new-ref/$filename; done | xargs ./scancode-toolkit/scancode -l -n 30 --json-pp - | grep short_name | sort | uniq >> new-licenses.txt
      # compare
      - name: License test
        run: if ! cmp old-licenses.txt new-licenses.txt; then echo "Licenses differ! Failing."; exit -1; else echo "Licenses are the same. Success."; exit 0; fi
```

### `pgproto3/notice_response.go`

Classification: Supplemental attribution / notice / patent text (not a standalone license determination)

```text
package pgproto3

type NoticeResponse ErrorResponse

// Backend identifies this message as sendable by the PostgreSQL backend.
func (*NoticeResponse) Backend() {}

// Decode decodes src into dst. src must contain the complete message with the exception of the initial 1 byte message
// type identifier and 4 byte message length.
func (dst *NoticeResponse) Decode(src []byte) error {
	return (*ErrorResponse)(dst).Decode(src)
}

// Encode encodes src into dst. dst will include the 1 byte message type identifier and the 4 byte message length.
func (src *NoticeResponse) Encode(dst []byte) ([]byte, error) {
	dst, sp := beginMessage(dst, 'N')
	dst = (*ErrorResponse)(src).appendFields(dst)
	return finishMessage(dst, sp)
}
```

### `web/static/vendor/D3-THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`

Classification: Reviewed bundled collection: BSD-3-Clause (content match, review scope); ISC (content match, review scope); MIT (content match, review scope); Mixed text: Apache-2.0, ISC (REVIEW); Mixed text: MIT, ISC (REVIEW); Unlicense (content match, review scope)

```text
D3 7.9.0 third-party licenses and pinned runtime dependency inventory
===================================================================

Scope: the complete published d3@7.9.0 dependencies closure, including the
D3 umbrella and every transitive runtime package. Development dependencies
are excluded. The full browser bundle is retained unchanged. The CLI-only
d3-dsv dependencies commander, iconv-lite, rw, and safer-buffer are included
conservatively for complete package-runtime closure, although they are not
imported by the browser entry points and are not bundled.

Primary metadata:
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/d3/d3/v7.9.0/package.json
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/d3/d3/v7.9.0/yarn.lock
https://registry.npmjs.org/d3/7.9.0

Method: resolve each published package.json dependencies selector against the
exact upstream Yarn v1 lock entry, download its exact npm tarball, and retain
all license/notice/copying files below verbatim. Each non-root tarball was
verified against both lockfile SRI and resolved SHA-1. The root tarball was
verified against npm version metadata SRI and SHA-1. SHA-256 values below
identify the downloaded archives. The root dist/d3.min.js was byte-compared
with the checked-in d3.v7.9.0.min.js. Package dependency edges are listed to
make graph closure independently auditable. No Node/npm commands were used.

Inventory: 38 packages (37 dependencies plus d3).
Static ESM import/export traversal: 34 browser-reachable packages, 566 source
modules. The other four packages are CLI-only, as identified above. This is
a source-entry reachability check, not a rebuild or post-minification module
manifest. Versions are the v7.9.0 lock pins, not inferred from minified code.
```
